{"forms":[{"form":"more presumptive","tags":["comparative"]},{"form":"most presumptive","tags":["superlative"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"adj","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"By late May, he was already considered his party’s presumptive nominee.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Based on presumption or conjecture; inferred, likely, presumed."]},{"examples":[{"text":"The Queen’s younger brother was the heir presumptive to the throne, until she gave birth to her first child.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Based on presumption or conjecture; inferred, likely, presumed.","Often postpositive, as in heir presumptive: of an heir or heiress: presumed to be entitled to inherit unless someone with a superior entitlement is born."]},{"glosses":["Based on presumption or conjecture; inferred, likely, presumed.","Of a cell or tissue: which has yet to differentiate, but is presumed to develop into a particular body part."],"topics":["embryology","medicine","sciences"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Forgive me for being presumptive, but aren’t you and Mark engaged?","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Synonym of presumptuous (“making unwarranted presumptions or assumptions, often out of arrogance or excessive self-confidence, and thus exceeding what is appropriate or right”)."],"synonyms":[{"word":"presuming"},{"tags":["synonym","synonym-of"],"word":"presumptuous"}]},{"glosses":["Chiefly in presumptive evidence: providing a reasonable basis for a certain presumption or conclusion to be drawn."],"tags":["often"],"topics":["law"]}],"sounds":[{"ipa":"/pɹɪˈzʌm(p)tɪv/","tags":["Received-Pronunciation"]},{"ipa":"/pɹiˈzʌm(p)tɪv/","tags":["General-American"]},{"ipa":"/pɹə-/","tags":["General-American"]}],"upstream_line":503568,"upstream_offset":2338338253,"upstream_record_sha256":"c1943c7fab034fd90d347ce6cc9b1fa200010e8d21b4e96bf2335fffc8722851","word":"presumptive"}
